    "?set_new_handler@@YAP6AXXZP6AXXZ@Z
    "Go 'way!", the witch doctor barks, "you know all I know."
    "I have a riddle for you," the Sphinx says.  "Answer correctly, and you shall be rewarded.  Answer incorrectly, and you shall be eaten.  Do you accept the challenge?"
    "The Dwarfbane!!!!, run for your lives."
    "You guessed incorrectly," the Sphinx says, smiling.  The Sphinx swipes at you with a paw, knocking you to the ground.  Another blow makes the world go black, and you know no more.

    'Grouped' combat formation bunches your army together in the center of your side of the battlefield.
    'Resurrects' creatures from a damaged or dead undead unit permanently.
    'Spread' combat formation spreads your armies from the top to the bottom of the battlefield, with at least one empty space between each army.

    A Black Dragon will take out a Titan any day of the week.
    A Horde of
    A Legion of
    A Nomad trader seeks protection from a tribe of Goblins.  For your assistance, he gives you a finely crafted pair of boots made from the softest leather.  Looking closely, you see fascinating ancient carvings engraved on the leather.
    A Swarm of
    A Throng of
    A Troll stops you and says: "Pay me 5,000 gold, or the Sword of Anduran will slay you where you stand."  You refuse.  The troll grabs the sword hanging from its belt, screams in pain, and runs away.  Picking up the fabled sword, you give thanks that half-witted Trolls tend to grab the wrong end of sharp objects.
    A blacksmith working at the foundry offers to convert all Pikemen and Swordsmen's weapons brought to him from iron to steel. He also says that he knows a process that will convert Iron Golems into Steel Golems.  Unfortunately, you have none of these troops in your army, so he can't help you.
    A brief roadside encounter with a small caravan and a game of knucklebones wins a magic pendant.  Its former owner says that it protects from Necromancers' death spells.
    A brief stop at a hastily abandoned Wizard's tower turns up a magical vial of mercury that always has a little left on the bottom.  Recognizing a treasure when you see one, you cap it and slip it in your pocket.
    A brief stop at an improbable rural inn yields an exchange of money, tales, and accidentally, luggage.  You find a magical skullcap in your new backpack.
    A chance meeting with a famous Archer finds you in a game of knucklebones pitting his bow against your horse.  You win.
    A clumsy Giant has killed himself with his own flail.  Knowing your superior skill with this weapon, you confidently remove the spectacular flail from the fallen Giant.
    A colorful Rogues' wagon stands empty here.  Perhaps more Rogues will be here later.
    A cry of pain leads you to a Centaur, caught in a trap.  Upon setting the creature free, he hands you a small pouch.  Emptying the contents, you find a dazzling jeweled ring.
    A dirty shovel has been thrust into a dirt mound nearby. Upon investigation, you discover it to be the enchanted shovel of the Gravediggers, long thought lost by mortals.
    A drink at the oasis fills your troops with strength and lifts their spirits.  You can travel a bit further today.
    A drink at the spring is supposed to give you twice your normal spell points, but you are already at that level.
    A drink at the watering hole fills your troops with strength and lifts their spirits.  You can travel a bit further today.
    A drink at the well is supposed to restore your spell points, but you are already at maximum.
    A drink from the spring fills your blood with magic!  You have twice your normal spell points in reserve.
    A drink from the well has restored your spell points to maximum.
    A face forms in the water for a moment, and then is gone.
    A few Trolls remain, cowering under the bridge.  They approach you and offer to join your forces as mercenaries.  Do you want to buy any Trolls?
    A fierce windstorm reveals the entrance to a buried tomb.  Your investigation reveals that the tomb has already been looted, but the thieves overlooked an ankh on a silver chain in the dark.
    A former Captain of the Guard admires your quest and gives you the enchanted Sword Breaker that he relied on during his tour of duty.
    A gang of Rogues is sifting through the possessions of dead warriors.  Scaring off the scavengers, you note the Rogues had overlooked a beautiful breastplate.
    A generous tip for the barkeep yields the following rumor:
    A glint of golden light catches your eye.  Upon further investigation, you find a golden helm hidden under a bush.
    A glorious victory!
    A group of Archers with a desire for greater glory wish to join you. Do you accept? 
    A group of Centaurs with a desire for greater glory wish to join you. Do you accept? 
    A group of Dwarves with a desire for greater glory wish to join you. Do you accept? 
    A group of Goblins with a desire for greater glory wish to join you. Do you accept?
    A group of Halflings with a desire for greater glory wish to join you. Do you accept? 
    A group of Orcs with a desire for greater glory wish to join you. Do you accept? 
    A group of Peasants with a desire for greater glory wish to join you. Do you accept? 
    A group of Skeletons with a desire for greater glory wish to join you. Do you accept? 
    A group of Sprites with a desire for greater glory wish to join you. Do you accept? 
    A group of tattered tents, billowing in the sandy wind, beckons you.  Do you wish to have any Nomads join you during your travels?
    A group of tattered tents, billowing in the sandy wind, beckons you.  The tents are unoccupied.  Perhaps more Nomads will be here later.
    A kindly Sorceress thinks that your army's defenses could use a magical boost.  She offers to enchant the Broach that you wear on your cloak, and you accept.
    A little leprechaun dances gleefully around a magic sack.  Seeing you approach, he stops in mid-stride.  The little man screams and stamps his foot ferociously, vanishing into thin air.  Remembering the old leprechaun saying 'Finders Keepers', you grab the sack and leave.
    A merchant from far away lands trades you a new invention of his people for traveling supplies.  It makes distant objects appear closer, and he calls it...
    A miniature view of the known world.  Left click to move viewing area.
    A multi-player game, with several human players competing against each other on a single map.
    A noblewoman, separated from her traveling companions, asks for your help.  After escorting her home, she rewards you with a bag filled with gold.
    A pack of
    A quick and deadly battle with a Necromancer wins you his magical pendant.  Later, a Wizard tells you that the pendant protects undead under your control from holy word spells.
    A rain of rocks strikes an area of the battlefield, damaging all nearby creatures.
    A retired captain living on this refurbished fishing platform offers to sell you maps of the sea he made in his younger days for 1,000 gold.  Do you wish to buy the maps?
    A second drink at the well in one day will not help you.
    A short rainstorm brings forth a rainbow...and you can see the end of it.  Riding quickly, you seize the pot of gold you find there.  The leprechaun who owns it, unable to stop you from taking it, offers an endless pouch of gems for the return of his gold.  You accept.
    A signpost reads:}
    A single player game playing out a single map.
    A single player game playing through a series of maps.
    A stone statue of a warrior holds a silver shield.  As you remove the shield, the statue crumbles into dust.
    A surprise turn in the landscape finds you in the midst of a grisly scene:  Vultures picking at the aftermath of a terrible battle.  Your cursory search of the remains turns up an enchanted hourglass.
    A traveling merchant offers you a rabbit's foot, made of gleaming silver fur, for safe passage.  The merchant explains the charm will increase your luck in combat.
    A traveling tinker in need of supplies offers you a helm with a thunderbolt design on its top in exchange for food and water.  Curious, you accept, and later find out that the helm is magical.
    A visit and a prayer at the temple raises the morale of your troops.
    A walk across a dry saltwater lake bed yields an unlikely prize:  A white pearl amidst shattered shells and debris.
    A whirlpool engulfs your ship.  Some of your army has fallen overboard.

    Advance
    Advanced
    Advanced Archery increases the damage done by range attacking creatures by 25 percent.
    Advanced Ballistics gives your hero's catapult an extra shot, and each shot has a greater chance to hit and do damage to castle walls.
    Advanced Diplomacy allows you to negotiate with monsters who are weaker than your group.  Approximately 1/2 of the creatures may offer to join you.
    Advanced Eagle Eye gives your hero a 30 percent chance to learn any given 3rd level spell (or below) used against him in combat.
    Advanced Leadership +2
    Advanced Leadership increases your hero's troops' morale by 2.
    Advanced Logistics increases your hero's movement points by 20 percent.
    Advanced Luck +2
    Advanced Luck increases your hero's luck by 2.
    Advanced Mysticism regenerates three of your hero's spell points per day.
    Advanced Navigation increases your hero's movement points over water by 2/3.
    Advanced Necromancy allows 20 percent of the creatures killed in combat to be brought back from the dead as Skeletons.
    Advanced Pathfinding reduces the movement penalty for rough terrain by 50 percent.
    Advanced Scouting increases your hero's viewable area by 2 squares.
    Advanced Wisdom allows your hero to learn fourth level spells.
    Advent
    Adventure
    After freeing a princess of a neighboring kingdom from the evil clutches of despicable slavers, she awards you with a Medal of Honor.
    After rescuing a Sorceress from a cursed tomb, she rewards your heroism with an exquisite jeweled necklace.
    After saving a young boy from a vicious pack of Wolves, you return him to his father's manor.  The grateful nobleman awards you with a Medal of Courage.
    After scouring the area, you fall upon a hidden treasure cache.  You may take the gold or distribute the gold to the peasants for experience.  Do you wish to keep the gold?
    After spending hours trying to fish the chest out of the sea, you open it and find 1500 gold pieces.
    After spending hours trying to fish the chest out of the sea, you open it, only to find it empty.
    Ahh, an adventurer! I will be happy to teach you a little of what I have learned over the ages for a mere 10 gems."  (Just bury it around my roots.)
    Air Summoning Altar
    Alamar
    Alchemist Lab
    Alchemist's Tower
    All dwellings increase population.
    All populations are halved.
    All the creatures will join us...
    All the dwarf towns have fallen.  This is a disastrous defeat!  You have lost.
    Allows the caster to magically transport to a nearby location.
    Allows the caster to view detailed information on enemy Heroes.
    Allows the computer to fight out the battle for you.
    Allows you to customize the combat screen.
    Alongside the remains of a burnt witch lies a beautiful broach, intricately designed.  Approaching the corpse with caution, you add the broach to your inventory.
    Ambrose
    Ammo Cart
    An Orcish witch doctor living in the hut deepens your knowledge of magic by showing you how to cast stones, read portents, and decipher the intricacies of chicken entrails.
    An ammunition cart in the middle of an old battlefield catches your eye.  Inspection shows it to be in good working order, so  you take it along.
    An earthquake will do you no good unless there are town walls to damage.
    An ensnared Unicorn whinnies in fright.  Murmuring soothing words, you set her free.  Snorting and stamping her front hoof once, she gallops off.  Looking down you see a golden horseshoe.
    An icicle withstanding the full heat of the noonday sun attracts your attention.  Intrigued, you break it off, and find that it does not melt in your hand.
    An improved version of fireball, fireblast affects two hexes around the center point of the spell, rather than one.
    An old Knight appears on the steps of the gazebo. "I am sorry, my liege, I have taught you all I can."
    An old Knight appears on the steps of the gazebo. "My liege, I will teach you all that I know to aid you in your travels."
    An old man claiming to be an inventor asks you to try his latest invention.  He then hands you a compass.
    An old sea captain is being tortured by Ogres.  You save him, and in return he rewards you with a wondrous instrument to measure the distance of a star.
    An old seaman tells you a tale of an enchanted masthead that he used in his youth to rally his crew during times of trouble.  He then hands you a faded map that shows where he hid it.  After much exploring, you find it stashed underneath a nearby dock.
    An unusual alliance of Orcs, Ogres, and Dwarves offer to train (upgrade) any such troops brought to them.  Unfortunately, you have none with you.

    Artifacts
    Artifacts:
    As the sirens sing their eerie song, your small, determined army manages to overcome the urge to dive headlong into the sea.
    As you approach the Centaurs' cave you notice that there is no one here.
    As you approach the Orcs' watch tower you notice that there is no one here.
    As you approach the Peasant dwelling you notice that there is no one here.
    As you approach the Skeletons' excavation you notice that there is no one here.
    As you approach the Sprites' tree house you notice that there is no one here.
    As you approach the bubbling pit of mud, creatures begin to climb out and position themselves around it.  In unison they say: "Mother Earth would like to offer you a few of her troops.  Do you want to recruit Earth Elementals?"
    As you approach the dwelling, you notice that there is no one here.
    As you approach the group of elves, their leader calls them all to attention.  He shouts to them, "Who of you is brave enough to join this fearless ally of ours?"  The group explodes with cheers as they run to join your ranks.
    As you approach the habitat you notice that there is no one here.
    As you approach the stables, the head groom appears, leading a fine looking war horse. "This steed will help speed you in your travels. Alas, he will grow tired in a week. You must also let me give better horses to your mounted soldiers, their horses look shoddy and weak."
    As you approach the stables, the head groom appears, leading a fine looking war horse. "This steed will help speed you in your travels. Alas, his endurance will wane with a lot of heavy riding, and you must return for a fresh mount in a week. We also have many fine war horses which could benefit mounted soldiers, but you have none we can help."
    As you are walking along a narrow path, a nearby bush suddenly bursts into flames.  Before your eyes the flames become the image of a beautiful woman.  She holds out a magnificent sword to you.
    As you drink the sweet water, you gain luck for your next battle.
    As you enter the Alchemist's Tower, a hobbled, graying man in a brown cloak makes his way towards you.  He checks your pack, and sees that you have 1 cursed item.  For 750 gold, the alchemist will remove it for you.  Do you pay?
    As you speak the magic word, the glowing barrier dissolves into nothingness.

    At last, you have the opportunity and the facilities to rid the Empire of the necromancer's evil. Eradicate them completely, and you will be sung as a hero for all time.

    Experience
    Experience allows your heroes to go up levels, increasing their primary and secondary skills.
    Expert
    Expert Archery increases the damage done by range attacking creatures by 50 percent.
    Expert Ballistics gives your hero's catapult an extra shot, and each shot automatically destroys any wall, except a fortified wall in a Knight town.
    Expert Diplomacy allows you to negotiate with monsters who are weaker than your group.  All of the creatures may offer to join you.
    Expert Eagle Eye gives your hero a 40 percent chance to learn any given 4th level spell (or below) used against him in combat.
    Expert Leadership +3
    Expert Leadership increases your hero's troops' morale by 3.
    Expert Logistics increases your hero's movement points by 30 percent.
    Expert Luck +3
    Expert Luck increases your hero's luck by 3.
    Expert Mysticism regenerates four of your hero's spell points per day.
    Expert Navigation doubles your hero's movement points over water.
    Expert Necromancy allows 30 percent of the creatures killed in combat to be brought back from the dead as Skeletons.
    Expert Pathfinding eliminates the movement penalty for rough terrain.
    Expert Scouting increases your hero's viewable area by 3 squares.
    Expert Wisdom allows your hero to learn fifth level spells.

    In Remote Main
    In a dazzling display of daring, you break into the local jail and free the hero imprisoned there, who, in return, pledges loyalty to your cause.
    In reward for helping his cart out of a ditch, a traveling potion salesman gives you a "magic" gold watch.  Unbeknownst to him, the watch really is magical.
    In the middle of a patch of dead and dry vegetation, to your surprise you find a healthy green four-leaf clover.
    In your travels, you find a leather purse filled with gold that once belonged to a great warrior king who had the ability to transform any inanimate object into gold.
    In your wanderings you come across a hermit living in a small, tidy hut.  Impressed with your mission, he takes time out from his meditations to bless and give you a charm against curses.

    Play a Hot Seat game, where 2 to 4 players play around the same computer, switching into the 'Hot Seat' when it is their turn.
    Play a direct connect game, where 2 players use their own computers directly connected through their serial port by a null modem.
    Play a modem game, where 2 players use their own computers connected over the phone lines using modems.
    Play a network game, where 2 players use their own computers connected through a LAN (Local Area Network).
    Play with 2 human players, and optionally, up to 4 additional computer players.
    Play with 3 human players, and optionally, up to 3 additional computer players.
    Play with 4 human players, and optionally, up to 2 additional computer players.
    Play with 5 human players, and optionally 1 computer player.
    Play with 6 human players.

    Responding to cries for help, you find river Sprites making a sport of dunking an old man.  Feeling vengeful, you rescue the man and drag a Sprite onto dry land for awhile.  The Sprite, uncomfortable in the air, gives you a magic pendant to let him go.
    Responding to the panicked cries of a damsel in distress, you discover a young woman fleeing from a hungry bear.  You slay the beast in the nick of time, and the grateful Sorceress weaves a magic cloak from the bear's hide.
    Restless spirits of long dead warriors seeking their final resting place offer to join you in hopes of finding peace.  Do you wish to recruit ghosts?

    The Ammo Cart provides endless ammunition for all your troops that shoot.
    The Ankh doubles the effectiveness of all your resurrect and animate spells.
    The Arcane Necklace of Magic increases your spell power by 4.
    The Archmages dispel all good spells
    The Arena guards turn you away.
    The Arm of the Martyr increases your spell power by 3 but adds the undead morale penalty.
    The Armored Gauntlets of Protection increase your defense skill by 1.
    The Ballista of Quickness lets your catapult fire twice per combat round.
    The Battle Garb of Anduran combines the powers of the three Anduran artifacts.  It provides maximum luck and morale for your troops and gives you the Town Portal spell.
    The Black Pearl increases your spell power and knowledge by 2 each.
    The Book of Elements doubles the effectiveness of all your summoning spells.
    The Breastplate increases your defense by 5.
    The Captain's Quarters provides a captain to assist in the castle's defense when no hero is present.
    The Caster's Bracelet of Magic increases your spell power by 2.
    The Castle improves town defense and increases income to 1000 gold per day.
    The City of the Dead is empty of life, and empty of unlife as well.  Perhaps some undead will move in next week.
    The Coliseum provides inspiring spectacles to defending troops, raising their morale by two during combat.
    The Crown
    The Crystal Ball lets you get more specific information about monsters, enemy heroes, and castles nearby the hero who holds it.
    The Defender Helm of Protection increases your defense skill by 1.
    The Demon leaps upon you and has its claws at your throat before you can even draw your sword.  "Your life is mine," it says.  "I will sell it back to you for 2,500 gold."
    The Demon screams its challenge and attacks!  After a short, desperate battle, you slay the monster and receive 1,000 experience points and 2,500 gold.
    The Demon screams its challenge and attacks!  After a short, desperate battle, you slay the monster and receive 1,000 experience points.
    The Divine Breastplate of Protection increases your defense skill by 3.
    The Dragon Sword of Dominion increases your attack skill by 3.
    The Dragon city has no Dragons willing to join you this week.  Perhaps a Dragon will become available next week.
    The Dragon city is willing to offer some Dragons for your army for a price.  Do you wish to recruit Dragons?
    The Dungeon increases the income of the town by 500 / day.
    The Dwarves need conquering before they can interfere in King Archibald's plans.  Roland's forces have more than one hero and many towns to start with, so be ready for attack from multiple directions.  You must capture all of the enemy towns and castles to claim victory.
    The Elemental Ring halves the casting cost of all summoning spells.
    The Elven Lands
    The Empire is weak in this region. You will be unable to completely subdue all forces in this area, so take what you can before reprisal strikes. Remember, your true goal is to claim the Helmet of Anduran.
    The Enchanted Hourglass extends the duration of all your spells by 2 turns.
    The Endless Bag of Gold provides you with 750 gold per day.
    The Endless Cart of Ore provides 1 unit of ore per day.
    The Endless Cord of Wood provides 1 unit of wood per day.
    The Endless Pouch of Crystal provides 1 unit of crystal/day.
    The Endless Pouch of Gems provides 1 unit of gems per day.
    The Endless Pouch of Sulfur provides 1 unit of sulfur per day.
    The Endless Purse of Gold provides you with 500 gold per day.
    The Endless Sack of Gold provides you with 1000 gold per day.
    The Endless Vial of Mercury provides 1 unit of mercury per day.
    The Epic Battle
    The Eternal Scrolls
    The Evercold Icicle causes your cold spells to do 50% more damage to enemy troops.
    The Everhot Lava Rock causes your fire spells to do 50% more damage to enemy troops.
    The Evil Eye reduces the casting cost of curse spells by half.
    The Fire Cloak halves all damage your troops take from fire spells.
    The Fizbin of Misfortune greatly decreases your morale.
    The Foremost Scroll of Knowledge increases your knowledge by 5.
    The Fortifications increase the toughness of the walls, increasing the number of turns it takes to knock them down.
    The Four-Leaf Clover increases your luck in combat.
    The Gambler's Lucky Coin increases your luck in combat.
    The Gauntlet
    The Giant Flail of Dominion increases your attack skill by 1.
    The Giant's Pass
    The Gold Watch doubles the effectiveness of your hypnotize spells.
    The Golden Goose brings in an income of 10,000 gold per turn.
    The Golden Horseshoe increases your luck in combat.
    The Helmet increases your spell power by 5.
    The Heroes 2 Expansion CD-ROM is not in the drive.  Without a Heroes 2 Expansion CD-ROM you will only be able to play as the guest in a multi-player game.  If you have the CD, then exit the program, put the CD in, and try again.
    The Hideous Mask prevents all 'wandering' armies from joining your hero.
    The Holy Hammer increases your attack skill by 5.
    The Holy Pendant makes all your troops immune to curse spells.
    The Host already has a game in progress and is not accepting new players.
    The Host is not responding.  Keep waiting?
    The Ice Cloak halves all damage your troops take from cold spells.
    The Kinetic Pendant makes all your troops immune to paralyze spells.
    The Left Turret provides extra firepower during castle combat.
    The Legendary Scepter adds 2 points to all attributes.
    The Library increases the number of spells in the Guild by one for each level of the guild.
    The Lightning Helm halves all damage your troops take from lightning spells.
    The Lightning Rod causes your lightning spells to do 50% more damage to enemy troops.
    The Lucky Rabbit's Foot increases your luck in combat.
    The Mage Guild allows heroes to learn spells and replenish their spell points.
    The Mage's Ring of Power increases your spell power by 2.
    The Magic Book enables you to cast spells.
    The Major Scroll of Knowledge increases your knowledge by 3.
    The Marketplace can be used to convert one type of resource into another.  The more marketplaces you control, the better the exchange rate.
    The Masthead boosts your luck and morale by 1 each in sea combat.
    The Medal of Courage increases your morale.
    The Medal of Distinction increases your morale.
    The Medal of Honor increases your morale.
    The Medal of Valor increases your morale.
    The Minor Scroll of Knowledge increases your knowledge by 2.
    The Moat slows attacking units.  Any unit entering the moat must end its turn there and becomes more vulnerable to attack.
    The Nomad Boots of Mobility increase your movement on land.
    The Pendant of Death makes all your troops immune to holy spells.
    The Pendant of Free Will makes all your troops immune to hypnotize spells.
    The Pendant of Life makes all your troops immune to death spells.
    The Power Axe of Dominion increases your attack skill by 2.
    The Power Ring returns 2 extra spell points/turn to your hero.
    The Rainbow increases the luck of the defending units by two.
    The Right Turret provides extra firepower during castle combat.
    The Sailors' Astrolabe of Mobility increases your movement on sea.
    The Seeing-eye Pendant makes all your troops immune to blindness spells.
    The Serenity Pendant makes all your troops immune to berserk spells.
    The Shipyard allows ships to be built.
    The Shrine increases the necromancy skill of all your necromancers by 10 percent.
    The Shrouded Isles
    The Skullcap halves the casting cost of all mind influencing spells.
    The Snake Ring halves the casting cost of all your bless spells.
    The Sorceress' guild of Noraston has requested Roland's aid against an attack from Archibald's allies.  Capture all of the enemy castles to win, and don't lose Noraston, or you'll lose the scenario.  (Hint:  There is an enemy castle on an island in the ocean.)
    The Southern War
    The Spade gives you increased necromancy skill.
    The Sphere of Negation artifact is in effect for this battle, disabling all combat spells.
    The Sphere of Negation disables all spell casting, for both sides, in combat.
    The Sphinx asks you the following riddle:
    The Spiked Helm increases your attack and defense skills by 1 each.
    The Spiked Shield increases your attack and defense skills by 2 each.
    The Staff of Wizardry boosts your spell power by 5.
    The Statue increases your town's income by 250 per day.
    The Stealth Shield of Protection increases your defense skill by 2.
    The Storm adds +2 to the power of spells of a defending spell caster.
    The Superior Scroll of Knowledge increases your knowledge by 4.
    The Sword Breaker increases your defense by 4 and attack by 1.
    The Sword increases your attack skill by 5.
    The Tavern increases morale for troops defending the castle.
    The Tax Lien costs you 250 gold pieces/turn.
    The Telescope increases the amount of terrain your hero reveals when adventuring by 1 extra square.
    The Tent provides workers to build a castle, provided the materials and the gold are available.
    The Thieves' Guild provides information on enemy players.  Thieves' Guilds can also provide scouting information on enemy towns.  Additional Guilds provide more information.
    The Thunder Mace of Dominion increases your attack skill by 1.
    The Traveler's Boots of Mobility increase your movement on land.
    The True Compass of Mobility increases your movement on land and sea.
    The Ultimate Book of Knowledge increases your knowledge by 12.
    The Ultimate Cloak of Protection increases your defense skill by 12.
    The Ultimate Crown increases each of your basic skills by 4 points.
    The Ultimate Shield increases your attack and defense skills by 6 each.
    The Ultimate Staff increases your spell power and knowledge by 6 each.
    The Ultimate Sword of Dominion increases your attack skill by 12.
    The Ultimate Wand of Magic increases your spell power by 12.
    The Unknown Hero
    The Wand of Negation protects your troops from the Dispel Magic spell.
    The Wayward Son
    The Well increases the growth rate of all dwellings by 2 creatures per week.
    The White Pearl increases your spell power and knowledge by 1 each.
    The Witch's Broach of Magic increases your spell power by 3.
    The Wizard's Hat increases the duration of your spells by 10 turns!
    The above spells are available here.
    The above spells have been added to your book.
    The barbarian despot in this area is, as yet, ignorant of your presence. Quickly, build up your forces before you are discovered and attacked! Secure the region by subduing all enemy forces.
    The bones of Lord Slayer are buried in the foundation of the arena.
    The butler admits you to see the master of the house.  He trains you in the four skills a hero should know.
    The butler opens the door and looks you up and down.  "You are neither famous nor diplomatic enough to be admitted to see my master," he sniffs.  "Come back when you think yourself worthy."
    The captain sighs.  "You don't have enough money, eh?  You can't expect me to give my maps away for free!"
    The creatures are swayed by your diplomatic tongue, and make you an offer:
    The creatures are willing to join us!
    The current game has been saved as 'PLYREXIT'. Do you wish to keep playing with the computer filling in for the other humans?
    The currently selected map is not a valid choice for your next scenario.
    The dark side is stronger.
    The description of the currently selected map.
    The difficulty rating reflects a combination of various settings for your game.  This number will be applied to your final score.
    The dragons see you and call out.  "Our alliance with Archibald compels us to join you.  Unfortunately you have no room.  A pity!"  They quickly scatter.
    The dragons, snarling and growling, agree to join forces with you, their 'Ally'.
    The drink at the oasis is refreshing, but offers no further benefit.  The oasis might help again if you fought a battle first.
    The drink at the watering hole is refreshing, but offers no further benefit.  The watering hole might help again if you fought a battle first.
    The dwarves hail you, "Any friend of Roland is a friend of ours.  You may pass."
    The dwarves recognize their allies and gladly join your forces.
    The elves stand at attention as you approach.  Their leader calls to you and says, "Let us not impede your progress, ally!  Move on, and may victory be yours."
    The end of the world is near.
    The enemy has fled!
    The enemy has surrendered!
    The enemy is beaten.  Your side has triumphed!
    The entrance to the cave is dark, and a foul, sulfurous smell issues from the cave mouth.  Will you enter?
    The fabled golden bow of the elves lies here in the dust.  You take it and journey back to the elven towns.  They shower you with their graciousness and the king promises that his people will aid you whenever you seek help.
    The guest waits for the host to call and set up the game.
    The guest waits for the host to set up the game, then is automatically added in.  There can be multiple guests for TCP/IP and IPX games, but only 1 guest for NetBIOS games.
    The guest waits for the host to set up the game.
    The head groom approaches you and speaks, "You already have a fine horse, and have no inexperienced cavalry which might make use of our trained war horses."
    The head groom speaks to you, "That is a fine looking horse you have. I am afraid we can give you no better, but the horses your cavalry are riding look to be of poor breeding stock. We have many trained war horses which would aid your riders greatly. I insist you take them."
    The host has canceled the game.
    The host sets up the game options, chooses the number to dial, and places the call.
    The host sets up the game options.
    The host sets up the game options.  There can only be one host per network game.
    The jailer tells you that the hero who was imprisoned here has been released by the king who imprisoned him.
    The keeper of the mill announces: "Milord, I am sorry, there are no resources currently available. Please try again next week."
    The keeper of the mill announces: "Milord, I am sorry, there is no gold currently available.  Please try again next week."
    The keeper of the mill announces: "Milord, I have been working very hard to provide you with these resources, come back next week for more."
    The keeper of the mill announces: "Milord, I have been working very hard to provide you with this gold, come back next week for more."
    The lean-to is long abandoned.  There is nothing of value here.
    The lighthouse is now under your control, and all of your ships will now move further each turn.
    The local lords refuse to swear allegiance to Roland, and must be subdued.  They are wealthy and powerful, so be prepared for a tough fight.  Capture all enemy castles to win.
    The location of the great library has been discovered! You must make your way to it, and reclaim the city of Chronos in which it lies.
    The magical, soothing beauty of the Mermaids reaches you and your crew.  Just for a moment, you forget your worries and bask in the beauty of the moment.  The mermaids charms bless you with increased luck for your next combat.
    The map difficulty of the currently selected map.  The map difficulty is determined by the scenario designer.  More difficult maps might include more or stronger enemies, fewer resources, or other special conditions making things tougher for the human player.
    The mermaids silently entice you to return later and be blessed again.
    The mirror image is destroyed!
    The name of the currently selected map.
    The ogres give you a grunt of recognition, "Archibald's allies may pass."
    The ogres recognize you as the Dwarfbane and lumber over to join you.
    The pit of mud bubbles for a minute and then lies still.
    The rotting hulk of a great pirate ship creaks eerily as it is pushed against the rocks.  Do you wish to search the ship?
    The rotting hulk of a great pirate ship creaks eerily as it is pushed against the rocks.  Do you wish to search the shipwreck?
    The sorceresses to the northeast are rebelling! For the good of the empire you must quash their feeble uprising on your way to the mountains.
    The sounds of combat draw you to the scene of a fight between an old Barbarian and an eight-headed Hydra.  Your timely intervention swings the battle in favor of the man, and he rewards you with a pendant he used to use to calm his mind for battle.
    The spring only refills once a week, and someone's already been here this week.
    The targeted creatures are paralyzed, unable to move or retaliate.
    The three Anduran artifacts magically combine into one.
    The truth is out there.
    The two positions selected can not be swapped.
    There are 4 possible loss conditions, as indicated by the following icons:
    There are 6 possibilities:
    There are seven resources in Heroes 2, used to build and improves castles, purchase troops and recruit heroes.  Gold is the most common, required for virtually everything.  Wood and ore are used for most buildings.  Gems, Mercury, Sulfur and Crystal are rare magical resources used for the most powerful creatures and buildings.
    There are two enemies allied against you in this mission.  Both are well armed and seek to evict you from their island.  Avoid them and capture Dragon City to win.
    There is no open space adjacent to your hero to summon an Elemental to.
    There is no room in the garrison for this army.
    There once was a man from Nantucket...
    These weak creatures will surely flee before us.

    This is the final battle against your rival kingdom of Harondale. Eliminate everyone, and don't lose the hero Jarkonas VI.
    This is the final battle.  Both you and your enemy are armed to the teeth, and all are allied against you.  Capture Archibald to end the war!
    This is the final battle.  Both you and your enemy are armed to the teeth, and all are allied against you.  Capture Roland to win the war, and be sure not to lose Archibald in the fight!
    This item can't be traded.
    This lets you change player starting positions and colors.  A particular color will always start in a particular location.  Some positions may only be played by a computer player or only by a human player.
    This lets you change the class of a player.  Classes are not always changeable.  Depending on the scenario, a player may receive additional towns and/or heroes not of their primary alignment.
    This lets you change the starting difficulty at which you will play.  Higher difficulty levels start you off with fewer resources, and at the higher settings, give extra resources to the computer.

    This town may not be upgraded to a castle.
    This window provides information on the status of your hero or kingdom, and shows the date.  Left click here to cycle through these windows.

    Toggle the hex grid on or off.  The hex grid always underlies movement, even if turned off.  This switch only determines if the grid is visible.
    Toggle the monster info window, which shows information on the active and targeted monsters.

    Where could it be?
    While out scaring up game, your troops find a mysterious staff levitating about three feet off of the ground.  They hand it to you, and you notice an inscription.  It reads: "Brains best brawn and magic beats might.  Heed my words, and you'll win every fight."
    While pausing to rest, you notice a bobcat climbing a short tree to get at a crow's nest.  On impulse, you climb the tree yourself and scare off the cat.  When you look in the nest, you find a collection of shiny stones and a ring.
    While searching through the rubble of a caved-in mine, you free a group of trapped Dwarves.  Grateful, the leader gives you a golden bracelet.
    While venturing into a decrepit hut you find the Skeleton of a long dead witch.  Investigation of the remains reveals a glass eye rolling around inside an empty skull.
    While waiting out a storm, a lighting bolt strikes a nearby cottage's lightning rod, which melts and falls to the ground.  The tip of the rod, however, survives intact and makes your hair stand on end when you touch it.  Hmm...
    Whirlpool
    White Pearl
    White stone pillars support a roof that rises up to the sky.  As you enter the structure, the dead air of the outside gives way to a whirling gust that almost pushes you back out.  The air current materializes into a barely visible form.  The creature asks, in what can only be described as a loud whisper: "Why have you come?  Are you here to call upon the forces of the air?"

    You and your ally 
    You are unable to recruit at this time, your ranks are full.
    You beat the Ghosts and are able to restore the mine to production.
    You can't afford to upgrade your troops!
    You cannot pick up this artifact, you already have a full load!
    You catch a leprechaun foolishly sleeping amidst a cluster of magic mushrooms.  In exchange for his freedom, he guides you to a small pot filled with precious things.
    You come across a conjurer who begs to accompany you and your army awhile for safety.  You agree, and he offers as payment a copy of the book of the elements.
    You come across a giant Sphinx.  The Sphinx remains strangely quiet.
    You come across a golem wearing a glowing pendant and blocking your way.   Acting on a hunch, you cut the pendant from its neck.  Deprived of its power source, the golem breaks down, leaving you with the magical pendant.
    You come across a lavish shrine attended by a group of high priests.  In exchange for your protection, they agree to teach you a sophisticated spell - 
    You come across a small shrine attended by a group of novice acolytes.  In exchange for your protection, they agree to teach you a simple spell - 
    You come across an old wagon left by a trader who didn't quite make it to safe terrain.  Inside, you find some of the wagon's cargo still intact.
    You come across an old wagon left by a trader who didn't quite make it to safe terrain.  Unfortunately, others have found it first, and the wagon is empty.
    You come across an ornate shrine attended by a group of rotund friars.  In exchange for your protection, they agree to teach you a spell - 
    You come across the remains of an ancient Druid.  Bones, yellowed with age, peer from the ragged folds of her robe.  Searching the robe, you discover a scroll hidden in the folds.
    You come upon a battle where a Paladin has been mortally wounded by a group of Zombies.  He asks you to take his hammer and finish what he started.  As you pick it up, it begins to hum, and then everything becomes a blur.  The Zombies lie dead, the hammer dripping with blood.  You strap it to your belt.
    You come upon a bridge spanning a dry gully.  Before you can cross, a Troll steps out from under the bridge and demands payment before it will permit you to pass.  You refuse, and the Troll charges, forcing you to slay it.  You take its spiked shield as a trophy.
    You come upon a caravan of gypsies who are feasting and fortifying their bodies with mead.  They call you forward and say "If you prove that you can dance the Rama-Buta, we will reward you."  You don't know it, but try anyway.  They laugh hysterically, but admire your bravery, giving you a Crystal Ball.
    You come upon a sign.  It reads: "Here lies the body of Anduran.  Bow and swear fealty, and you shall be rewarded."  You decide to do as it says.  As you stand up, you feel a coldness against your skin.  Looking down, you find that you are suddenly wearing a gleaming, ornate breastplate.
    You come upon a very old woman, long blind from cataracts and dying alone.  You tend to her final needs and promise a proper burial.  Grateful, she gives you a magic pendant emblazoned with a stylized eye.  It lets you see with your eyes closed.
    You come upon an abandoned gold mine.  The mine appears to be haunted.  Do you wish to enter?
    You come upon an ancient artifact.  As you reach for it, a pack of Rogues leap out of the brush to guard their stolen loot.
    You come upon an obelisk made from a type of stone you have never seen before.  Staring at it intensely, the smooth surface suddenly changes to an inscription.  The inscription is a piece of a lost ancient map.  Quickly you copy down the piece and the inscription vanishes as abruptly as it had appeared.
    You come upon the pyramid of a great and ancient king.  Routine exploration reveals that the pyramid is completely empty.
    You come upon the pyramid of a great and ancient king.  You are tempted to search it for treasure, but all the old stories warn of fearful curses and undead guardians.  Will you search?
    You come upon the remains of an unfortunate adventurer.  Searching through the tattered clothing, you find 
    You come upon the remains of an unfortunate adventurer.  Searching through the tattered clothing, you find nothing.
    You don't have enough gold!
    You don't have enough memory for non-interlaced video.
    You drink from the enchanted fountain, but nothing happens.
    You encounter the infamous Black Knight!  After a grueling duel ending in a draw, the Knight, out of respect, offers you a pair of armored gauntlets.
    You enter a recently burned glade and come upon a Fire Elemental sitting atop a rock.  It looks up, its flaming face contorted in a look of severe pain.  It then tosses a glowing object at you.  You put up your hands to block it, but it passes right through them and sears itself into your chest.
    You enter a rickety hut and talk to the magician who lives there.  He tells you of places near and far which may aid you in your journeys.
    You enter the arena and face a pack of vicious lions.  You handily defeat them, to the wild cheers of the crowd.  Impressed by your skill, the aged trainer of gladiators agrees to train you in a skill of your choice.
    You enter the faerie ring, but nothing happens.
    You enter the structure of white stone pillars, and find nothing.
    You find a powerful and grotesque Demon in the cave.  "Today," it rasps, "you will fight and surely die.  But I will give you a choice of deaths.  You may fight me, or you may fight my servants.  Do you prefer to fight my servants?"
    You find a small tree that closely resembles the great Warlock Carnauth with a ring around one of its twigs.  Scraps of clothing and rotting leather lead you to suspect that it IS Carnauth, transformed.  Since you can't help him, you take the magic ring.
    You find an elaborate container which houses an old vellum scroll. The runes on the container are very old, and the artistry with which it was put together is stunning. As you pull the scroll out, you feel imbued with magical power.
    You gain control of a crystal mine. It will provide you with one unit of crystal per day.
    You gain control of a gem mine. It will provide you with one unit of gems per day.
    You gain control of a gold mine. It will provide you with 1000 gold per day.
    You gain control of a sawmill. It will provide you with two units of wood per day.
    You gain control of a sulfur mine. It will provide you with one unit of sulfur per day.
    You gain control of an ore mine. It will provide you with two units of ore per day.
    You have already been to this obelisk.
    You have already cast a spell this round.
    You have been eliminated from the game!!!
    You have captured a mischievous imp who has been terrorizing the region.  In exchange for his release, he rewards you with a magical coin.
    You have captured an enemy artifact!
    You have failed to complete your quest in time.  All is lost.
    You have no hero to cast a spell.
    You have no room to carry another artifact!
    You have received quite a bargain.  I expect to make no profit on the deal.  Can I interest you in any of my other wares?
    You have taken control of the local Alchemist shop. It will provide you with one unit of Mercury per day.
    You have your crew stop up their ears with wax before the sirens' eerie song has any chance of luring them to a watery grave.
    You hear a voice from behind the locked door, "You don't have enough gold to pay for my services."
    You hear a voice from high above in the tower, "Go away! I can't help you!"
    You may only summon one type of elemental per combat.
    You meet an old Wizard friend of yours traveling in the opposite direction.  He presents  you with a gift:  A wand that prevents the use of the dispel magic spell on your allies.
    You must be standing on the entrance to a mine (sawmills and alchemists don't count) to cast this spell.
    You must be within 3 spaces of a monster for the Visions spell to work.
    You must purchase a spell book to use the mage guild, but you currently have no room for a spell book.  Try giving one of your artifacts to another hero.
    You must put down a peasant revolt led by Roland's forces.  All are allied against you, but you have Lord Corlagon, an experienced hero, to help you.  Capture all enemy castles to win.
    You must take control of the castle of Magic, where the fount of wizardry lies. Do this and your victory will be supreme.
    You must unify the barbarian tribes of the north by conquering them.  As in the previous mission, the enemy is not allied against you, but they have more resources at their disposal.  You will win when you own all of the enemy castles and there are no more heroes left to fight.
    You own Dragon City.
    You own the Lighthouse.
    You pause to help a diplomat with a broken axle fix his problem.  In gratitude, he gives you a writing quill with magical properties which he says will "help people see things your way".
    You search the ruins, but the Medusas that used to live here are gone.  Perhaps there will be more next week.
    You search through the flotsam and find some wood and some gold.
    You search through the flotsam, and find some wood and some gold.
    You search through the flotsam, and find some wood.
    You search through the flotsam, but find nothing.
    You see a Wizard fleeing from a Griffin and riding like the wind.  The Wizard opens a portal and rides through, getting his hat knocked off by the edge of the gate.  The Griffin follows; the gate closes.  You pick the hat up, dust it off, and put it on.
    You see a silver axe embedded deeply in the ground.  After several unsuccessful attempts by your army to remove the axe, you tightly grip the handle of the axe and effortlessly pull it free.
    You speak, and nothing happens.
    You spy a gleaming object poking up out of the ground.   You send a member of your party over to investigate.  He comes back with a golden helmet in his hands.  You realize that it must be the helmet of the legendary Anduran, the only man who was known to wear solid gold armor.
    You stand before the Dragon City, a place off-limits to mere humans.  Do you wish to violate this rule and challenge the Dragons to a fight?
    You stop to help a Peasant catch a runaway mare.  To show his gratitude, he hands you a tiny sphere.  As soon as you grasp it, you feel the magical energy drain from your limbs...
    You stumble upon a dented and tarnished lamp lodged deep in the earth. Do you wish to rub the lamp?
    You stumble upon a medal lying alongside the empty road.  Adding the medal to your inventory, you become aware that you have acquired the undesirable Fizbin of Misfortune, greatly decreasing your army's morale.
    You tentatively approach the burial ground of ancient warriors.  Do you want to search the graves?
    You try to pay the leprechaun, but realize that you can't afford it.  The leprechaun stamps his foot and ignores you.
    You visit an alchemist who, upon seeing your army, is swayed by the righteousness of your cause.  The newly loyal subject gives you his endless pouch of sulfur to help with the war effort.
    You will face four allied enemies in a straightforward fight for resources and treasure.  Capture all of the enemy castles for victory.
    You've come upon a fight between a Necromancer and a Paladin.  The Necromancer blasts the Paladin with a fire bolt, bringing him to his knees.  Acting quickly, you slay the evil one before the final blow.  The grateful Paladin gives you the fire cloak that saved him.
    You've come upon a mercenary camp practicing their tactics.  "You're too advanced for us," the mercenary captain says.  "We can teach nothing more."
    You've come upon a mercenary camp practicing their tactics.  The mercenaries welcome you and your troops and invite you to train with them.
    You've found a Goblin weapon smithy making weapons for use against humans.  With a tremendous yell you and your army descend upon their camp and drive them away.  A search finds a magic ore cart that never runs out of iron.
    You've found a Sprite Tree City.  Unfortunately, none of the Sprites living there wish to join an army.  Maybe next week.
    You've found a group of Druids worshipping at one of their strange stone edifices.  Silently, the Druids turn you away, indicating they have nothing new to teach you.
    You've found a group of Druids worshipping at one of their strange stone edifices.  Silently, they teach you new ways to cast spells.
    You've found a magic garden, the kind of place that leprechauns and faeries like to cavort in, but there is no one here today.  Perhaps you should try again next week.
    You've found an abandoned lean-to.  Poking about, you discover some resources hidden nearby.
    You've found an ancient and weathered stone idol.  It is supposed to grant luck to visitors, but since the stars are already smiling upon you, it does nothing.
    You've found an ancient and weathered stone idol.  Kissing it is supposed to be lucky, so you do.  The stone is very cold to the touch.
    You've found an oddly shaped ring on the finger of a long dead traveler.  The ring looks like a snake biting its own tail.
    You've found one of those bridges that Trolls are so fond of living under, but there are none here.  Perhaps there will be some next week.
    You've found some Medusas living in the ruins.  They are willing to join your army for a price.  Do you want to recruit Medusas?
    You've found the ruins of an ancient city, now inhabited solely by the undead.  Will you search?
    You've pulled a shipwreck survivor from certain death in an unforgiving ocean.  Grateful, he says, "I would give you an artifact as a reward, but you're all full."
    Your answer?
    Your army is ambushed by a small tribe of wild (and none too bright) Orcs.  You fend them off easily and the survivors flee in all directions.  One of the Orcs was wearing a polished spiked helm.  Figuring it will make a good souvenir, you take it.
    Your attack skill is a bonus added to each creature's attack skill.
    Your big spending habits have earned you a massive tax bill that you can't hope to pay.  The tax man takes pity and agrees to only take 250 gold a day from your account for life.  Check here if you want one dollar to go to the presidential campaign election fund.
    Your cowardly forces flee from battle.
    Your defense skill is a bonus added to each creature's defense skill.
    Your enemies are allied against you and start close by, so be ready to come out fighting.  You will need to own all four castles in this small valley to win.
    Your forces suffer a bitter defeat.
    Your forces surrender to the enemy, and depart in shame.
    Your hero is too tired to cast this spell today.  Try again tomorrow.
    Your hero produces 100 gold pieces per turn as tax revenue from estates.
    Your hero produces 250 gold pieces per turn as tax revenue from estates.
    Your hero produces 500 gold pieces per turn as tax revenue from estates.
    Your knowledge determines how many spell points your hero may have.  Under normal cirumstances, a hero is limited to 10 spell points per level of knowledge.
    Your looting of the grave of Sinfilas Gardolad, the famous shapeshifting Warlock, unearths his fabled mask.  Trembling, you put it on and it twists your visage into an awful grimace!  Oh no!  It's actually the hideous mask of Gromluck Greene, and you are stuck with it.
    Your men spot a navigational buoy, confirming that you are on course and increasing their morale.
    Your men spot a navigational buoy, confirming that you are on course.
    Your mission is to vanquish the warring mages in the magical Shrouded Isles. The completion of this task will give you a fighting chance against your rivals.
    Your orders are to conquer the country lords that have sworn to serve Roland.  All of the enemy castles are unified against you.  Since you start without a castle, you must hurry to capture one before the end of the week.  Capture all enemy castles for victory.
    Your remote connection to the other players appears to be broken.  Do you wish to keep waiting for a response?
    Your rival, the kingdom of Harondale, is attacking weak towns on your border! Recover from their first strike and crush them completely!
    Your spell power determines the length or power of a spell.
    Your task is to defend the Dwarves against Archibald's forces.  Capture all of the enemy towns and castles to win, and be sure not to lose all of the dwarf towns at once, or the enemy will have won.
    Your troops can be upgraded, but it will cost you dearly.  Do you wish to upgrade them?
    Your wanderings bring you into contact with a tribe of ape-like beings using a magical lava rock that never cools to light their fires.  You take pity on them and teach them to make fire with sticks.  Believing you to be a god, the apes give you their rock.
    Zombie
    Zounds
